People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. I just kind of lose the thread of it without meaning to.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself acting impulsively or having trouble waiting your turn, like interrupting others when they’re speaking or blurting out answers before someone finishes a question?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do actually. I really don't mean to be rude, but... well, at work, if I hear my coworkers trying to figure out a customer problem, I'll just jump right in. I end up giving an answer before they've even finished talking, or before anyone actually asked me for help. I just sort of blurt it out without thinking.\n\nDoctor: Do these difficulties with focusing and acting impulsively happen in other places besides work, like at home or when you're with friends or family?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it happens at home, too. Even with quiet stuff that's supposed to relax me. Like, if I try reading at home or sitting down to watch a movie with my wife, it doesn't last long. I'll start shifting around, making comments, or finding a reason to get up and do something else. I just can't seem to stay settled.\n\nDoctor: When did you first start noticing these difficulties with focusing and staying still? Were they something you experienced as a child, or did they start more recently?\n\nPatient: Well, looking back, this has been around long before this job change. It's definitely something I experienced as a kid. I remember back in grade school, my teachers would write on my report cards that I was bright but careless. They always noted that I talked out of turn and constantly had to be reminded to just stay with my work. So, yeah, it's been going on for a long time.\n\nDoctor: When you were a child, did these difficulties with focusing and staying still happen in places other than school, like at home or with friends?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure about that. I mean, it's been a long time, and I mainly just remember what the teachers used to say on my report cards. I don't have a clear memory of how it was at home or with friends back then.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall if these difficulties with focusing and staying still were noticeable before you turned 12 years old?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. Like I mentioned a minute ago about my grade school report cards, that was well before I turned 12. So, yes, all that stuff the teachers wrote about me being careless and talking out of turn—that was happening back then. It's been around long before any of my recent jobs.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or as if you need to be constantly moving, even in situations where others typically remain still, like during meetings, meals, or quiet activities?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. It's like... if I have to sit still through a training video at work, or if I'm stuck in a slow appointment somewhere, I start feeling kind of boxed in. I just get this strong urge where I feel like I need to stand up, stretch, or at least be doing something with my hands.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself forgetting daily tasks or getting easily distracted by things around you, even when you're trying to focus?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Especially with my job. I'll... well, I'll mean to call a customer back, or maybe answer an internal message right after my lunch break. But honestly, unless I write it down in more than one place, it can just completely slip my mind until the next day.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. They always noted that I talked out of turn and constantly had to be reminded to just stay with my work. I mean, it's been a long time, and I mainly just remember what the teachers used to say on my report cards. I don't have a clear memory of how it was at home or with friends back then.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall if these difficulties with focusing and staying still were noticeable before you turned 12 years old?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. Like I mentioned a minute ago about my grade school report cards, that was well before I turned 12. So, yes, all that stuff the teachers wrote about me being careless and talking out of turn—that was happening back then. It's been around long before any of my recent jobs.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or as if you need to be constantly moving, even in situations where others typically remain still, like during meetings, meals, or quiet activities?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. It's like... if I have to sit still through a training video at work, or if I'm stuck in a slow appointment somewhere, I start feeling kind of boxed in. I just get this strong urge where I feel like I need to stand up, stretch, or at least be doing something with my hands.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself forgetting daily tasks or getting easily distracted by things around you, even when you're trying to focus?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Especially with my job. I'll... well, I'll mean to call a customer back, or maybe answer an internal message right after my lunch break. But honestly, unless I write it down in more than one place, it can just completely slip my mind until the next day.\n\nDoctor: Have these difficulties with focus, restlessness, and forgetfulness caused you any significant problems in your job, relationships, or other important areas of life?\n\nPatient: Yeah, they have. At work, it's actually started showing up in my performance reviews. My supervisors have been pointing out that I leave tickets unfinished, or I make careless mistakes that end up slowing the whole team down. It's frustrating because I'm really trying hard, and I generally get along great with the customers, but the focus issues are definitely causing problems there.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
